## Document 012: 20260914_投資週報_地緣風險升溫物價維持高檔， 市場聚焦Fed會議

Source PDF: all report pdf/20260914_投資週報_地緣風險升溫物價維持高檔， 市場聚焦Fed會議.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同
意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 14 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國 8 月 CPI 公布後，市場對聯準會政策路徑的關注進一步升溫：美伊衝突持續升溫帶動市
場對原油供給的擔憂，推升國際油價維持高檔震盪，能源價格上漲也使通膨再度成為市場關
注焦點。美國 8 月 CPI 年增率為 3.4%，與前月持平，但月增率由 0.1%升至 0.4%；核心 CPI
年增率則降至 2.4%。此次數據顯示通膨回落進程並未逆轉，但能源成本攀升仍可能增加未來
物價壓力。儘管聯準會理事表示，若通膨維持改善趨勢，9 月傾向按兵不動，但油價上漲是

否影響後續降通膨進程仍有待觀察，使利率政策路徑與市場預期持續出現分歧。
◼ AI 基建需求持續擴張，台灣 8 月出口再創歷史新高：台灣 8 月出口達 824 億美元、年增
41%，刷新歷史紀錄，其中電子零組件出口首度突破 300 億美元，年增 58%。AI 伺服器、
高階晶片、資料中心設備與雲端建設需求持續強勁，成為出口與科技產業成長的核心動能。
◼ 胡賽武裝威脅帶動西德州油價突破 100 美元大關：胡賽武裝 9 月 8 日攻擊沙國能源設施，9
月 10 日再奪取鄰近曼德海峽的摩卡港，推升能源風險溢價。由於沙國已將部分原油改經紅海
延布港出口，亞洲航線仍須通過曼德海峽。胡賽勢力逼近關鍵航道，即使尚未封鎖海峽，也可
能降低航商與保險業者承運意願，增添紅海供應中斷疑慮，帶動西德州油價突破 100 美元。
市場展望
◼ AI 題材擴散，科技股中期向上趨勢料不變：近期股市修正主要來自美債殖利率攀升壓縮高評
價科技股現值，半導體族群股價表現承壓；然而，AI 變現題材接棒，資金由晶片端轉向軟體應
用層，預料 AI 應用題材將持續發酵。建議降低高評價、高擁擠與高度依賴遠期獲利族群的集

中度，此外，投資人可採取定期定額或是趁股價拉回時分批佈局具獲利潛力的台日股市。
◼ 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債為債市首選：美國財政部擴大長天期公債回購 ， 一度帶動長端
殖利率回落，但通膨與需求數據仍具韌性，且就業市場表現穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度
升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨仍要求較高風險補償，考量當前 3～10 年
期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收 ， 且其受長端財政風險影響也相對較小 ， 故現階段
3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17
貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

市場反應與投資建議
9/16
市場反應
• 市場將華許記者會解讀為偏鷹，那斯達克指數、標普
500指數、道瓊工業指數皆下跌。
• 10 年 期 美 債 殖 利 率 在 利 率 決 議 公 布 後 一 度 降 至
4.9323%，隨後回升至接近 4.99%，在關鍵的5%關
口附近波動。
• 2年期殖利率則由盤中低點4.5975%升至4.7124%，
反映投資人重新提高對後續升息的預期。
• 黃金跌破4,260美元。
• 利率市場顯示 ，交易員加大 聯準會在2026 年底前進
一步升息的押注，12月升息機率升至約50%。

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。

顧 • AI安全監管升溫，短期壓抑市場情緒：Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度，並引入獨立第三方
評估等安全機制，引發市場對AI資本支出降溫的疑慮。不過，前沿模型開發速度放緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。
Alphabet、微軟及亞馬遜近期AI相關積壓訂單持續攀升，合計達約1.64兆美元，其中微軟及Alphabet分別突破6,000億及
5,000億美元，搭配雲端業務持續成長，顯示企業AI需求仍具韌性，相關訂單有望逐步轉化為未來數年的營收與獲利。
• 槓桿壓力逐步消化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值：美國雲端巨頭擴大AI資本支出，壓抑短期自由現金流；相較之下，亞洲
科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。
新興市場本幣債券各因子對報酬率貢獻
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

顧
新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。
新興市場本幣債券存續期間的貢獻度
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。
布局全球多重資產，掌握成長契機
各類資產收益率(股息率或殖利率)
資料來源: 凱基投信 2026.8.1

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本

貳、公 司 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
日成控股成立於 1989 年，資本額 4.90 億元，並於 2017 年掛牌
上市，日成控股為流行珠寶及飾品設計製造出口企業，初期公司
名為日成有限公司。 1991 年公司更名為日成珠寶製造股份有限公
司，經過數年的經營，生產規模擴大，生產工藝及設計能力深受
國際客戶喜愛，並在當地珠寶及飾品設計製造產業享負盛名。有
鑑於公司成長及管理需求，積極推動專業管理方式，於 2003 獲得  
ISO 9001 認證。另，於 2004 年導入  ERP 系統，以因應公司未
來業務需求，快速成長趨勢，資源整合，以達有效管理及運用。
科技日新月異，日成公司為滿足客戶要求，不斷增強研發能力，
於 2007 年大量投入具有較強的設計能力及使用最先進的技術 ， 已
拓展生產能方面機器，如沖壓鑄造、金屬多樣鑄臺，並軟體以及
使用 3D 設計技術軟件等，設計出時尚與滿足客戶需求之產品。

二. 2026 年營運概況及展望  
日成 -KY 主要營業項目為珠寶首飾設計、製造與銷售，產品包含
吊墜、戒指、耳環、手環、手鍊、項鍊與服飾配件等，主要生產
基地位於泰國曼谷近郊的龍仔厝，基地面積 6.59 萬平方米，員工

際精品大廠，銷售區域遍布歐美亞三大洲，且日成控股泰國工廠
規模符合國際品牌代工條件，標準化生產製程具效率，且具有
ODM 設計開發能力。此外，公司也是泰國少數同時具有大規模產
能及電鍍產線的 ODM 代工廠。

圖二、珠寶產業分佈

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

觀察全球主要珠寶品牌商受到白銀價格大幅上漲侵蝕利潤與下游
終端客戶需求，因此將加速鉑金  (Platinum) 系列的轉型與推廣，
以多樣化產品線應對成本波動。為降低對白銀的依賴，計畫將部
分暢銷產品從銀製改為鍍鉑金設計，同時將產品線中銀製品的比
例從約 60% 降至 20% ，並希望在 2028 年前完成此轉型。由於鉑
金價格遠高於白銀，因此會在合金基底上鍍鉑金，既可減少鉑金
使用量，也有助控制成本，最終售價與銀製品相同，將有助廠商
降低銀價上漲侵蝕利潤的情況。

研 究  報 告                
天鈺(4961.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
下半年逆風成⻑，營運優於上半年  
 投資評價與建議 
考量公司下半年營收仍逆風成⻑，營運優於上半年，且成功在部分產品進行漲
價來轉嫁成本，毛利率有望回升，給予PER 16倍(以2027年EPS評價)。 
 重點評論分析 
預計3Q26營運成⻑動能延續，推升單季營收季增9% ：預計3Q26營收季增
8.5%，主要受惠市佔率擴大與全產品線新品量產，帶動8月單月營收達21.1億
元創三年來新高，3Q26 Mobile出貨將微幅下滑，佔比下降至32~37%，且最
競爭的TDDI毛利率已回升至健康水準 ， 非驅動IC產品線將會回升 ， 有望帶動毛
利率上升至27.4%，稅後EPS約3.09元。 
終端疲弱添變數，但下半年營收仍逆風成⻑、優於上半年：(1)雖然終端疲弱添
變數，但市佔率擴大與新品量產帶動下半年營收優於上半年，全新的手機
A M O L E D新產品預計於3 Q 2 6驗證導入(目前O L E D營收佔比約1 ~ 2 % )，
AMOLED筆電驅動IC預估4Q26量產，新⼀代EPD PMIC成功導入北美客戶機
種出貨 ， 2H26持續量產出貨 ， ESL開發E6多彩規格與GOA面板技術應用 ， Edge 
AI晶片訂單在智慧家居與生物識別領域放量，4Q26出貨量呈現翻倍成⻑
( Edge AI營收佔比約1%)。(2)自1Q26起，晶圓代工廠與封測廠等上游供應鏈
每季持續對客戶漲價，公司已成功在部分產品調漲以轉嫁成本，2H26整體毛
利率具備進步與回升的空間。 
 投資風險 
終端消費電子疲弱、晶圓代工廠與封測廠漲價導致成本上升。

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況  
Mobile IC 與大中尺寸顯示器驅動 IC 帶動 2Q26 營收季增 19.7%，預計 3Q26 營運動能延續推升營收季增 9%：
2Q26 營收 53.9 億元，營收季增 19.7%，主要係因 Mobile IC、大/中尺寸顯示器驅動 IC 與電源管理 IC 業務皆有
所成⻑，抵銷了 Other Semi 微幅下滑的波動，Mobile IC 第⼆季成⻑最為顯著，主要受惠手機、TFT/OLED IoT
產品與平板 TDDI 大量出貨，大/中尺寸顯示器驅動 IC 方面，則受惠公司打入新客戶，新產品量產與價格上揚帶動
整體營收提升(季成⻑動能依序為 Auto>Monitor>TV)，毛利率 26.9%，季減 0.9 個百分點，主要受生產成本上升
及毛利率最低的 Mobile 產品線出貨大幅增加，營收佔比上升至 38.6%，營業利益率 9.2%，季增 2.7 個百分點，
主要係因營業費用嚴格控管，單季歸屬⺟公司業主淨利為 3.84 億元，單季稅後 EPS 3.21 元。 
預計 3Q26 營運成⻑動能延續，推升單季營收季增 8.5%，受惠市佔率擴大與全產品線新品量產帶動，8 月單月營
收達 21.1 億元創三年來新高，3Q26 Mobile 佔比有望下降至 32~37%，且最競爭的 TDDI 毛利率已回升至健康
水準，預計帶動毛利率上升至 27.4%，稅後 EPS 約 3.09 元。 
終端疲弱添變數，下半年營收仍逆風成⻑、優於上半年：(1)市佔率擴大與新品量產帶動下半年營收優於上半年，
Mobile IC 方面，推出⼀系列 TDDI 新產品，目前正在客戶端快速驗證中，全新的手機 AMOLED 新產品亦預計
於 3Q26 驗證導入，AMOLED 筆電驅動 IC 預估 4Q26 量產，此外，受惠於燃油車與電動車內建螢幕數量持續增
加，車用驅動 IC 成⻑顯著，3Q26 佔比已升至高個位數百分比，成為中⻑期穩定的營運驅動引擎。手機相關 PMIC
於印度、中國擴張市佔帶動絕對⾦額上升，新⼀代 EPD PMIC 成功導入北美客戶機種出貨，2H26 持續量產出貨，
ASIC PMIC 預估 4Q26 完成工程樣品並於年底導入客製專案。其他相關半導體產品線方面，ESL 開發 E6 多彩規
格與 GOA 面板技術應用，T-Con 具備獨家色彩強化演算法，可節省外部 DRAM 成本，全新規格預估 4Q26 量
產，Edge AI 晶片訂單在智慧家居與生物識別領域放量，預估 4Q26 出貨量較 3Q26 呈現翻倍成⻑。(2)布局機器
人、無人機等新興科技領域，搶攻⻑期結構性成⻑趨勢。公司積極開發不同應用級別的邊緣運算，針對協作機器
人與機械手臂防護、Power 及 MCU 相關應用進行開發，無人機主打提供高性價比、平價的邊緣 AI 晶片，提供
2D/3D 視覺辨識的方案，亦參與經濟部關於機器人、無人機與低軌衛星的自主研發計劃。(3)雖然面臨晶圓代工與
封測漲價導致毛利率仍承壓，但積極與客戶溝通轉嫁成本：自 1Q26 起，晶圓代工廠與封測廠等上游供應鏈每季
持續對客戶漲價，公司已成功在部分產品調漲以轉嫁成本，2H26 整體毛利率具備進步與回升的空間。

### Page 3

3
| 公 司 簡 介
天鈺(4961)是顯示驅動 IC 與電源管理 IC 設計廠商 ， 產品線涵蓋大/中尺寸顯示器驅動 IC(Display IC) 、 中小尺寸
驅動 IC (Mobile IC)、電源管理 IC(Power IC)及時序控制與電子紙驅動等其他相關半導體(Other Semi)解決方案，
在電子紙驅動 IC 及電子貨架標籤(ESL)市占率全球第⼀。2Q26 產品組合為 Other Semi 26.81%、Mobile IC 38.64%、
Display IC 24.50%、Power IC 10.02%。主要客戶包含面板廠友達、京東方、華星光電、元太，主要競爭對手包
括聯詠、奇景、瑞鼎、敦泰、矽創。
天鈺的其他相關半導體(Other Semi)產品線整合了電子貨架標籤(ESL)、電子紙顯示器(EPD)驅動、Edge AI 晶片與
時序控制晶片(T-Con)技術，ESL 跟 EPD 廣泛應用於零售電子標籤跟電子紙顯示器，市占率約 80~90%為全球第
⼀， Edge AI 晶片應用於智慧家電、生物識別與 AI 玩具的控制，滿足邊緣運算的部署需求。大尺寸 LCD 顯示器

驅動 IC 主要應用於電視、桌上型顯示器及筆記型電腦等大尺寸面板，中尺寸用於車載與工控，天鈺在大/中尺寸
LCD 顯示器驅動 IC 市占率約 5~8%。小尺寸驅動 IC 廣泛應用於智慧型手機、平板電腦及穿戴式裝置，由於該市
場面臨嚴峻的價格壓力，其毛利率通常低於公司平均水準，公司產品線毛利率排序: Other Semi>大尺寸>公司平
均毛利率>小尺寸。為提升競爭門檻與獲利能力，天鈺積極進行產品線優化，PMIC 持續向非消費性、車用、工控
等高附加價值領域轉型，並積極開拓新產品與新市場，現有產品已擴張至印度、巴⻄等新興市場，應用層面涵蓋
直流無刷馬達、網通設備與智慧家電，此外，受惠於網通與家電轉向 Type-C 快充趨勢，其 PD 協議晶片出貨持續
增⻑，與客戶合作開發 ASIC PMIC 專案，預估於 4Q26 完成工程樣品，⻑線上規劃切入機器人、伺服器電源等高
毛利率市場，目前 Edge AI 佔比約 1%，OLED 營收佔比約 1~2%，未來將持續推升這兩大⻑線轉型產品的比例。

## Document 159: A changing investment narrative requires a careful reader (2026-09-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/A changing investment narrative requires a careful reader (2026-09-28).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #interestrates #businessconditions #credit
A changing investment narrative requires a careful reader
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:43 September 18 2026 Share
Business investment is now in the driver’s seat on growth, implying credit growth is a less
reliable guide to financial conditions more broadly.
Strong business investment, autonomous from government or central bank policy, has filled the gap
left by slower growth in public demand, and changed the narrative around the growth cycle. This has

implications for how we should think about financial conditions, and specifically business credit

### Page 2

Historically, much of the commentary on the growth cycle in Australia has been policy-driven. The RBA
sets the cash rate, which affects housing and mortgage markets, and so consumer spending. Business
investment was seen as largely downstream of a consumer-driven domestic demand cycle, on the logic
that businesses will not invest if demand is not there.

There were exceptions to this, most notably the rise of China and associated mining investment boom.
The pervasive effects of this boom and its unwind were systematically underestimated. Also not fully
appreciated at the time was the deadening effect of risk aversion and weak investment appetite in the
wake of the GFC. But these factors mostly played out over a longer horizon, and the shorter-run rates–
consumer nexus tended to be more in focus.

Another policy-driven narrative became more prominent in recent years, with public demand as the
swing variable. Government infrastructure projects and the growth in the care economy were driving
growth in domestic demand. Initially we worried that when the infrastructure projects ended and the
expansion in the care economy matured, the handover would be ‘shaky’ and growth momentum would
weaken further.

Fortunately, that did not happen. Developments in the private sector, autonomous from domestic policy,
have recently become a key driver and filled the gap as public sector demand growth slowed. In many
respects, the data centre boom and associated renewables spend is rewriting the narrative, and our
recent forecast revisions reflect this.

This shift has several implications for how we should think about financial conditions and specifically
how business credit growth fits into any assessment of financial conditions.

First, business investment is not that correlated with short-term interest rates. This was true in
the post-GFC period of slow growth, when hurdle rates stayed high even though interest rates were
unusually low. It will also be true in the upswing. The hyperscalers and other funders that are driving
the data centre boom are essentially indifferent to cost. They do not seem to care that they are funding
themselves at much wider spreads than similarly rated non-tech firms. Nor do they seem to care about
cost to build. They just want to get it done as fast as possible. But if their activities are impervious to
growth.
Typically, business investment is only loosely correlated with interest rates, and business credit
growth is in turn only loosely correlated with new investment. Business balance sheets strengthened
in the wake of the pandemic, so there might be scope for further increases in leverage. The pre-
pandemic period of low growth, inflation and interest rates is in any case not the relevant period of
comparison for where we are now.
If something is essentially impervious to interest rates, then it cannot be an indicator of financial
conditions more broadly. There is a difference between a claim that policy is restrictive but needs to
be even tighter because of a positive shock, and a claim that policy is not tight at all. These are
important considerations for how we should interpret developments in business credit.

### Page 3

interest rates and spreads, they cannot guide views on how the economy is responding to interest
rates.

Second, business investment is only loosely correlated with business credit quarter to quarter or
year to year. A stylised description of many businesses’ strategies is to use debt funding to acquire
assets or other companies, but use retained earnings to fund new investment. Short-run fluctuations in
business credit growth might therefore provide weak or misleading signals about investment or activity
more broadly. Moreover, if businesses’ funding mix changes, even medium-term shifts in business
credit growth might have little implication for underlying activity.

Third, balance sheets matter. Businesses came out of COVID cashed up, in part because of the fiscal
support they received during the pandemic. Business leverage – whether measured as debt relative to
financial assets broadly or debt to cash plus deposits – dipped below even the low post-GFC average.
It has since returned to that average but might have scope to increase further. This would not
necessarily mean credit supply has eased. Rather, the population of borrowers has effectively changed
and become stronger.

Fourth, and related to the previous point, the comparison period matters. We must recall that the
period between the GFC and the pandemic was the anomaly – low growth, low inflation, low
investment – and extremely low interest rates. The global structure of interest rates is likely to be higher
now and into the future, because of the AI boom, the US fiscal situation and the end of the post-GFC
inflation undershoot. But this is not news, and it is not necessarily a sign of a change in financial
conditions more recently.

This point is particularly salient for total credit growth as an indicator of financial conditions. Even a
return to inflation around or above target, rather than consistently below as in the 2010s, means that
average nominal credit growth rates from that period are not representative of “normal”. Nominal
growth in the economy was slower, and nominal credit growth is downstream from that. Moreover,
many of the factors that made the post-GFC period unusual – the weak risk appetite, regulatory
adjustments and balance sheet repair – would be expected to weigh on credit growth especially
heavily. There is also a cyclical element here: more (real) credit growth might not be saying anything
about credit supply, and so “financial conditions”. Rather, stronger growth makes for stronger borrowers
that are more willing to demand credit and more qualified to access it.

All of this suggests that observers should be cautious in how they interpret business credit conditions.
If something is essentially impervious to interest rates, then it cannot be an indicator of
financial conditions more broadly. To be fair, the hyperscalers are issuing bonds, not borrowing from
the domestic banking system and being recorded in business credit. To the extent that current business
investment and credit growth are being driven by the data centre boom’s broader spillovers to other
sectors, though, that provides almost no signal about the tightness of monetary policy.

### Page 4

Related articles
This is important given the apparent tendency of some recent RBA and other commentary to conflate a
shock with a reason to revise the neutral rate higher. For example, in one recent speech, it was argued
that, because AI was a smaller shock for Australia than some other countries (debateable), long yields
would rise by less than elsewhere. This in turn would result in a lower exchange rate than otherwise,
necessitating higher interest rates. But the exchange rate depreciation is part of the transmission of
that differential response to different-sized shocks. It is not an independent outcome requiring that
interest rates need to be higher, actually. In the face of a positive shock, yes, policy rates would need to
be higher than if the shock had not occurred. But being a lesser beneficiary of that shock is not a
heads-I-hike-tails-your-rates-go-up double-whammy.

This framing is even more curious, when one considers that the RBA never took any signal from the
USD selloff earlier in the year. That selloff had nothing to do with conditions in Australia, but it surely
lifted the AUD exchange rate at the margin – at least against some trading partners – and therefore
was slightly disinflationary.

The broader lesson here is not that this time is different, but rather “last time” – the period between the
GFC and the pandemic – was different. It has also taken a while to normalise from the pandemic itself,
especially where balance sheets and financial behaviour are concerned. If we fail to understand this
recovery away from the post-GFC era, or the implications of more fleeting shocks for financial
behaviour, we risk misconstruing where the economy might be headed and where policy settings need
to be to achieve desired outcomes. The strength of business credit does not have the same
implications as similarly strong household credit would.

The boom in data centres and renewables is reshaping Australia’s economic narrative and needs to be
a consideration in policy settings. But care must be taken to avoid overstating its implications for
financial conditions. There are many good reasons to think that the long-run average for the global
structure of interest rates (and so neutral rates) will be higher in future than pre-pandemic. Current
strong growth in business credit is not necessarily one of them.

## Document 174: AI需求仍在狂飙！英伟达最新披露：供应承诺额暴增135%，Anthropic已签约超1800亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI需求仍在狂飙！英伟达最新披露：供应承诺额暴增135%，Anthropic已签约超1800亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

英伟达最新披露显⽰， AI 基础设施需求持续扩张： Anthropic 已签约 2.6 吉⽡算⼒，相关合
同总值超 1800 亿美元；英伟达供应承诺额从 1190 亿美元升⾄ 2790 亿美元，环⽐⼤增超
130% ，主要由内存采购驱动。数据强化 AI 需求持续性，但承诺向收⼊的转化仍需关注。
摘要：
英伟达最新披露的数据显⽰， AI 基础设施需求持续扩张，多项关键指标较上季度⼤幅跳升，为市
场提供了迄今最新的需求能⻅度参考。
英伟达披露， AI 初创公司 Anthropic 迄今已签约 2.6 吉⽡的 AI 基础设施算⼒，交付期延伸⾄ 2028
年，其跨多家云服务商（ CSP ）及新兴云平台（ Neocloud ）的合同总价值已超过 1800 亿美元。
与此同时，英伟达整体供应承诺额从上季度的 1190 亿美元⼤幅攀升⾄ 2790 亿美元，增幅超过
130% ，本次承诺额的增⻓主要与内存采购相关。
上述数据的披露延续了英伟达在财报周期中定期公布合同承诺规模的惯例。市场对此类数据的
解读通常遵循固定逻辑：合同总额被视为需求持续性的佐证，⽽承诺构成则决定其实际分量。

Anthropic 签约规模凸显头部 AI 客⼾需求强度
英伟达披露， Anthropic 已累计签约 2.6 吉⽡ AI 基础设施算⼒，交付期覆盖⾄ 2028 年，跨多家 CSP
及 Neocloud 平台的合同总价值超过 1800 亿美元。
这⼀数字直观呈现了头部 AI 实验室在算⼒资源上的⼤规模前置锁定意愿。从交付时间线来看，合
同覆盖⾄ 2028 年，意味着相关需求具备较⻓的可⻅窗⼝，有助于⽀撑市场对英伟达中期营收的
预期。
英伟达同时披露，其在澳⼤利亚的 Neocloud 合作伙伴承诺于 2027 年前交付最⾼ 2 吉⽡的算⼒，
进⼀步体现了 AI 基础设施需求向全球多个市场延伸的趋势。
承诺额季度环⽐翻倍，内存采购为主要驱动
英伟达整体供应承诺额从上季度的 1190 亿美元升⾄ 2790 亿美元，单季增幅达 1600 亿美元，英伟
达指出，本次承诺额增⻓主要与内存采购相关。
这⼀区分⾄关重要。市场分析⼈⼠通常将与终端算⼒需求直接挂钩的合同承诺，与以采购为导
向的供应链承诺区别对待。内存及封装等关键组件的⼤规模提前锁定，既可解读为对订单簿的

信⼼体现，也可视为在供应紧张环境下的对冲操作 —— 两种解读在需求假设出现松动时，对估
值的影响⽅向并不相同。
在过去组件供应受限的周期中，激进的内存采购承诺曾被市场同时赋予上述两种含义，投资者
需关注相关承诺最终转化为确认收⼊的⽐例、预付款及取消条款的披露情况，以及内存价格⾛
势能否验证采购逻辑。
投资组合持续扩张，需求叙事得到强化
英伟达当前投资组合涵盖 13 家上市公司及 229 家私营公司，客⼾⽣态的⼴度在⼀定程度上分散了
对单⼀⼤客⼾集中度的担忧。

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

## Document 185: Asset Tokenization 101_ The Next Phase of Financial Infrastructure (2026-09-16)

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY: Tokenization expands into market infrastructure
Market share of tokenized assets, Sep 14 2026
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Sep 14, 2026. “Distributed” denotes tokens
transferable outside the issuing platform and between wallets; it does not measure trading activity. Note:
Credit combines asset-backed credit, corporate credit, diversified credit and specialty finance. Other

assets comprise non-US government debt, private equity, venture capital and real estate.
– The global tokenized assets market, excluding stablecoins, grew from
~$10bn in Jan 2025 to ~$39bn in Sep 2026. US Treasury debt remains the
largest segment outside stablecoins, with money market funds leading
near-term adoption. Despite the US Digital Asset Market Clarity Act failing
to advance through to the Senate yesterday, we remain positive about the
momentum to tokenize both assets and capital markets in the coming

years.
– Institutions are increasingly applying tokenization to settlement, funding
and collateral management. Notable examples include DTCC’s tokenization
service, DL T-based intraday repo and collateral initiatives, and the
Eurosystem’s Pontes solution, which connects DL T platforms to TARGET
Services and is expected to launch later this month.
– A broad tokenized capital market requires progress across three areas:
assets, settlement money and market infrastructure. The tokenized RWA

market remains small relative to traditional markets, and token issuance
does not automatically create secondary-market liquidity.
– In this chartbook, we broaden our focus from tokenized assets, such as
bonds and stocks, to the infrastructure through which they are issued; and
review (i) market size; (ii) adoption trends; (iii) the regulatory drivers of
tokenization; and (iv) barriers that will determine the pace of growth.

– US Treasury debt remains the largest on-chain tokenized asset segment excluding stablecoins, reaching $15bn in September 2026. Much of
that is driven by tokenized money market funds (MMF).
– Demand reflects appetite for highly liquid, short-term risk-free collateral assets, with the ability to generate yield.

### Page 4

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
– Tokenization records assets or claims as digital tokens on a
distributed ledger, using public or permissioned networks.
– Potential benefits include faster transfers, automated servicing and
smaller investment units.
– Shared records can reduce reconciliation, but trading hours,
redemption terms and investor access remain product-specific.
Two issuance models:
– On-chain (or native): The security or fund share is issued directly on a

distributed ledger.
– Off-chain (or tokenized representation): The underlying asset
remains outside the blockchain, but ownership claims and economic
rights are represented digitally on-chain.

### Page 5

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
STABLECOIN SPOTLIGHT: The settlement side of tokenization
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s FX Special Report: Tokenised money and the future of FX -
Deutsche Bank Research
– Stablecoins account for ~89% of the combined value of stablecoin and
distributed tokenized assets. We assess them separately because their
principal role is payment and settlement.
– They support near-instant, round-the-clock transfers for remittances,

business payments and treasury operations. Cross-border savings depend on
transaction fees, FX costs, compliance and conversion into bank money.
– Chainalysis projects adjusted annual transfer volume could rise from $28tn in
2025 to $719tn by 2035; higher scenario approaching $1.5 quadrillion.
Regulation and competition
– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
The GENIUS Act takes effect on the earlier of 18 January, 2027, or 120 days

after final implementing regulations are issued.
– Stablecoins could increase competition for bank deposits and payments.
Exchange rewards are distinct from issuer-paid interest, which GENIUS
prohibits for permitted payment stablecoins.
Risks
– Trust depends on reserve quality, legal redemption rights and operational
resilience. Stablecoins remain dependent on their issuers and supporting
institutions.
Tokenized deposits and central bank money provide alternative settlement options. Connecting the cash and securities legs of a transaction can improve settlement

efficiency and reduce fragmentation.
For more on tokenized money and settlement, particularly for FX, read here.

interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?

market cap here I would say.

### Page 6

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
– Early efforts focused on tokenized claims on traditional securities ( i.e. BlackRock’s
BUIDL, Franklin Templeton FOBXX), while the underlying assets remained off -chain.
– Financial institutions are now extending tokenization to the infrastructure through
which assets are distributed, traded, settled and financed.
DLT adoption: small, but intraday repo offers a practical use case

– Global DLT fixed-income issuance reached €4.8bn in 2025, up 48% from 2024.
– DLT intraday repo (i.e. JPM Kinexys and Broadridge’s DLR) can accelerate collateral
transfers and settlement, helping institutions manage short-term funding needs.
– A Finadium study estimated that using intraday DLR for 15% of activity could reduce
intraday liquidity buffer needs by 8–17%.
Tokenized securities on blockchain rails
– DTCC to launch its tokenization service in Oct 2026, covering Russell 1000 constituents,

ETFs tracking major indices, and US Treasury bills, notes and bonds.
– Initial production trades took place in July with >30 participating firms. Tokens are
recorded on DTCC's Hyperledger Besu network or on Canton, settling on either. The
SEC granted a three-year authorization.
Official-sector initiatives and interoperability
– Project Agorá, led by the BIS and IIF with central banks and financial institutions,
demonstrated a prototype for cross-border settlement using tokenized deposits and

central bank reserves. Real-value testing is the next phase.
– Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a joint interoperability framework with
BCG in March 2026 to address fragmentation across digital asset markets.
– Eurosystem’s Pontes solution connects DLT platforms to TARGET Services for
settlement in central bank money.
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Tokenization expands into funding, settlement and
collateral

SHORT-TERM ADOPTION: Tokenized Treasuries and money market funds lead
Tokenized US Treasury market value reaches $15bn– Tokenized cash-management funds, including BUIDL and USYC,
combine short-term yields with more flexible transfers and collateral

use.
– Potential benefits include faster settlement and lower transaction costs,
making Treasury exposure more accessible across eligible digital
platforms.
– Direct Treasury bond tokenization accounted for 27% of overall US
Treasury tokenization in 2025, indicating adoption beyond fund
structures.
– These assets can also serve as collateral in repo and margin
arrangement, supporting demand beyond investment returns.
Secondary-market constraints
– Tokenized bonds are often held to maturity. Limited investor

participation and weak interoperability between platforms constrain
secondary-market liquidity.
– Some exchanges are attempting to improve liquidity by connecting
digital and conventional markets through dual listings, including SIX
digital exchange and Swiss Stock exchange).
Risks
– Redemption terms can differ from the settlement hours and liquidity of
underlying assets, creating potential liquidity mismatches.
– Blockchain transparency can improve visibility but may also accelerate

withdrawals during stress.
– Using tokenized fund shares across multiple products and as collateral
can create additional channels of contagion.
Largest providers of tokenized Treasury products

MEDIUM-TERM ADOPTION: Tokenized equities remain small; DTCC’s launch could
broaden adoption
Market value of tokenized equities
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data up to Sep 10, 2026.
– Tokenized equities remain small (<1%) relative to traditional stock markets.

– 68% of equity tokenization in 2025 consisted of direct tokenization of
individual stocks or baskets of stocks, vs. tokenized equity funds.
– Suggests that blockchain adoption is increasingly focused on the securities
themselves (i.e. DTCC method) rather than through third-party platforms (i.e.
Ondo and xStocks).
Developments
– DTCC’s tokenization service, scheduled to launch in Oct, will allow eligible
existing securities to move across blockchain infrastructure within its

regulated framework.
– These tokenized securities will retain the entitlements, investor protections
and ownership rights associated with their traditional form.
– NYSE has announced plans for a tokenized securities platform designed to
support 24/7 trading of US-listed equities and ETFs, with near-instant
settlement and stablecoin funding.
Benefits: Reduce barriers for foreign investors; automated post-trade processes
and longer trading hours; reduce information asymmetry (e.g., embedding listing

Risks & limitations
– Tokenized equities require clear legal ownership, enforceable investor rights
and alignment with existing securities rules.
– Under some third-party wrapper structures, the token provides exposure to a
share without making its holder a shareholder in the underlying company.
– Investors may therefore face issuer or custodian risk and may not receive the
same voting, dividend or other rights as direct shareholders.

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
LONG-TERM ADOPTION: Private credit expands through record-keeping; real
estate is growing fast but from a negligeable base
Real estate tokenization process
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Updated September 10. Excludes platforms that issues tokens using blockchain as a
recordkeeping layer ( i.e. Represented Layer)
Private credit
– Private credit stands ~$8bn in total, including asset-backed credit ($2.6bn),

specialty finance ($2.5bn), corporate credit ($1.9bn), and diversified credit
($798mn).
– Activity includes lending linked to digital assets and selected corporate credit
products.
– A larger use case is blockchain-based record-keeping: represented credit stands
at ~$35bn, over 4x the distributed market.
– Companies like Figure Technologies and Tradable use blockchains to support
loan administration, ownership records, payments tracking, and servicing.

Challenges
– Tokenization remains concentrated among a small number of issuers and lacks a
deep, and liquid secondary market.
– Legal fragmentation, credit quality and servicing arrangements remain
important risks. Blockchain records do not remove the underlying borrower’s
default risk.
Tokenized credit market stands at ~$8bnReal estate
– The market expanded from ~$35mn in 2025 to $226mn in Sep 2026, although it
remains small.
Benefits
– Fractional ownership can lower minimum investment sizes (typically for $100-

$1,000 per unit) and widen access, particularly where traditional capital markets
and mortgage markets are limited.
Challenges
– A BIS study found that liquidity improvements can depend on platforms offering
to buy tokens back. These arrangements can help investors exit but expose
platforms to liquidity and solvency risks.

Jurisdiction Regulatory status Main implication
EU MiCA + DLT pilot regime
Comprehensive crypto-assets regulation. Tokenized financial instruments and tokenized
deposits remain subject to securities and deposits regulation respectively (e.g. MiFID).
National pilots (e.g., EU’s DLT Pilot Regime) are testing tokenized capital-markets
applications; focus is on capital market infrastructure modernization.

UK Cryptoassets Regime / BoE Systemic
Stablecoins Regime Live - Digital
Securities Sandbox, Digital Gilt pilot
Program (DIGIT) and Great British
Tokenised Deposits (GBTD) Project
UK Cryptoassets and Stablecoins Regime finalised in 2026. FCA and BoE launched the Digital
Securities Sandbox for firms to test DLT-based blockchains; HSBC first bank to go live. The
FCA has also identified fund tokenization as a strategic priority through 2025-2030. FCA/BoE

launched joint vision for wholesale tokenised markets in 2026 – work ongoing. DIGIT / GBTD
development ongoing.
GENIUS Act to begin in Jan 2027 with
final rule out by Nov 26; CLARITY Act
stalling
GENIUS establishes a federal framework for stablecoins. Key controversy is whether yield or
reward mechanisms should be permitted in stablecoins and other digital assets; oversight is
fragmented across the SEC, CFTC, banking regulators and state authorities. CLARITY Act,

market structure bill, addressing yield proposal and related items failed its procedural vote in
Sep. Attention will refocus on US federal regulators for digital asset rules in absence of market
structure.
China opposes permissionless crypto, not
tokenization itself
Strict rules on cryptocurrencies remain; China’s focus remains on e-CNY and state-approved
blockchain networks. Recent reforms suggest the e-CNY is moving away from CBDC towards
a tokenised deposits model, reflecting limited retail CBDC uptake alongside existing mobile

payment platforms solutions.
Project Ensemble Sandbox (Aug 2024);
new regulatory roadmap for digital assets
(Feb 2025); progress ongoing.
HKMA has supported tokenized green bonds; tokenized deposits; tokenized money-market
and investment products. Project Ensemble has progressed to real-value transaction testing
using tokenised deposits. HK to announce a new digital bond platform in H22026 to support
issuance and settlement of tokenized bonds.
Project Guardian (c. 2022)

Institutional tokenization sandbox; money market funds and bonds are tokenized and settled
on-chain. Focus is increasingly on commercialisation and scaling successful pilot projects
across funds, bonds, deposits and FX.
Financial Instruments and Exchange Act,
overseen by the Financial Services
Agency
Legally recognizes security tokens through FIEA since 2020. Subjects security tokens (STOs)
to the same regulations as traditional securities, including registration and disclosure, custody

and investor protection obligations.
Abu Dhabi’s ADGM regulatory
framework treats security tokens as
regulated financial instruments.
Tokenized securities classified as “digital securities” under ADGM. Actively supports
blockchain-based projects that allow businesses to test tokenization ideas via its sandbox
program.
BARRIERS TO ADOPTION: Weak secondary market liquidity and lack of regulations
continue to challenge adoption
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research.

10
Main barriers to adoption
– Secondary-market liquidity remains low. Issuance
growth has not consistently translated into active
trading or deep markets.
– Regulatory uncertainty: Fragmented or incomplete
frameworks slow institutional adoption and complicate
integration with traditional financial infrastructure.
– Legal uncertainty persists over whether token
ownership conveys ownership of the underlying
asset or a claim on it.
– In segments like money market funds, rapid

cross-border growth can increase regulatory
arbitrage, capital-flow volatility and AML/CFT
compliance challenges. Policymakers must
adapt frameworks as the technology evolves.
– Fragmented infrastructure: Public blockchains,
permissioned institutional ledgers and hybrid platforms
often lack interoperability, fragmenting markets.
Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a joint
framework to address this in March 2026.
– Limited integration with existing custody, settlement,

and record-keeping arrangements prevents institutions
from scaling tokenized products.
– Proprietary infrastructure and centralized
controls also raise questions about ownership
and governance.
10

### Page 11

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon | ASSET TOKENIZATION 101 |  September 2026
OUTLOOK: Tokenized capital markets could become a core part of financial by the 2030s
Conservative forecasts for tokenized real-world
assets (ex stablecoins)
Source: Deutsche Bank Research.
– Tokenization will modernize financial infrastructure rather than eliminate intermediaries. We expect a gradual
transition onto digital rails, with banks retaining key roles across payments, FX, treasury, financing, collateral

and trading settlement.
Assumption, on the positive side reflects…
– So far, tokenization has achieved its greatest scale in stablecoins, with activity concentrated in crypto trading
and payments gaining ground.
– Short-term: Adoption will be driven by tokenized US Treasuries and money market funds, expanding as yield-
bearing collateral in repo and margin arrangements.
– Long-term:  The opportunity extends beyond assets to the infrastructure supporting issuance, settlement,

custody, collateral management and repo markets.
– We are seeing 3 growth catalysts: (i) infrastructure: major financial market infrastructures (DTCC, NYSE,
Nasdaq) are integrating tokenization into core workflows; (ii) liquidity: Growth of regulated on-chain money
(stablecoins and tokenized deposits) providing a trusted settlement foundation; (iii) and regulation: US 2025
GENIUS ACT continues to encourage global digital asset adoption, although the failure of the CLARITY act to

advance yesterday likely rules out progress in this congressional session until Jan 2027. Looking ahead, focus
will turn back to the SEC and CFTC for crypto rules.
…Risks to adoption
– Accelerated liquidity runs: Tokenization may create a new form of systemic liquidity risk that spreads faster than
traditional finance.
– Money market funds yield-chasing: Declining interest rates or rising competition may push MMF issuers into
risker assts, less-liquid assets to boost yield, undermining their safety.

– Crypto contagion: Tight integration with crypto markets via stablecoin reserves and collateral risks transmitting
digital asset volatility to conventional financial markets.
– Operational risks: Smart-contract vulnerabilities, cyberattacks, network outages could halt trading and prevent
access to the underlying assets.
– Financial Crime: Traditional institutions must scale up compliance and AML controls to manage illicit activities
on decentralized networks.

AUTHORS
Marion Laboure is a Managing Director and Macro Strategist at Deutsche Bank in London, and a Lecturer in Economics and Finance at
Harvard University.
She was previously an economist at the IMF, the Central Bank of Luxembourg, Barclays, and the European Commission. She has tw ice
received the First Prize of the American Society of Actuaries, was named a rising star in finance by Revue Banque, and is recognised among

the 45 standout women in fintech globally.
She holds a Bachelor's degree in Mathematics from the University of Paris Dauphine, a Master of Research in Economics from th e London
School of Economics, and a PhD in Econometrics and Quantitative Economics from the École Normale Supérieure.
She is the author of Democratizing Finance published by Harvard University Press.
Camilla Siazon is an Associate on Deutsche Bank’s Macro and Thematic team in London.

She focuses on digital assets, geopolitics, and global macroeconomic trends. Prior to Deutsche Bank, she worked at geopolitical advisory
firms Eurasia Group and Greenmantle.
She holds a dual MA/MSc in International History from Columbia University and the London School of Economics, and a BA in His tory from
Barnard College, Columbia University.

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we
1 Source: Bloomberg as of September 8, 2026.
2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

Investment Implications
The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal concerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic bouts
of market pressures, we view recent
move as a normalization process, not a
crisis of confidence. Attractive real and
nominal yields reinforce our view that
investors should look beyond cash and
short-duration assets and move closer

to strategic duration target.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

6 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to cconcerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to

other Government bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason for yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still above average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 203: CEE & CCA week ahead_ Inflation data from Poland and Czech GDP

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ Inflation data from Poland and Czech GDP.pdf

### Page 1

Article | 25 September 2026 1
THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article  |  25 September 2026   CZECH REPUBLIC   POLAND
CEE & CCA week ahead: Inflation data
from Poland and Czech GDP
Poland and Kazakhstan will release inflation data next week, while the
Czech Republic publishes second-quarter GDP details and September
industrial PMI figures
We estimate that headline CPI in Poland rose above 4.0% YoY in September
Poland: Inflation set to rise above 4%

Wednesday brings the release of the September CPI flash estimate. The end of the temporary
VAT cut on fuels (from 23% to 8%) in August, combined with higher crude oil prices in recent
weeks, pushed fuel costs to fresh highs in September.
Meanwhile, food prices likely remained broadly stable, as the earlier sharp declines in
vegetable prices appear to have run their course. Core inflation was probably little changed,
but we estimate that headline CPI rose above 4.0% YoY, thereby moving clearly above the

upper bound of the National Bank of Poland's inflation target tolerance range (2.5% +/-1
percentage point). This might support or even boost already high bets on rate hikes in Poland.
Czech Republic: GDP details and industrial PMI
The statistical office is expected to confirm the headline GDP figures for the second quarter on
Wednesday, while the breakdown may be revised, as the contribution of changes in
inventories may be absorbed into other expenditure categories. The main point of interest is

whether household consumption remains strong or shows any signs of weakness. The

### Page 2

THINK Economic and financial analysis
Article | 25 September 2026 2
industrial PMI likely softened in September, on the back of the Hormuz conflict re-escalation
that brought oil prices to new heights and increased uncertainty about future economic
performance on the global scale.
CCA: Kazakhstan inflation expected to ease on base effects
CPI data for Kazakhstan will be released on Thursday. We expect monthly inflation to remain
above its historical seasonal norm, continuing a pattern seen for much of this year. Rising

external food price pressures, lingering pass-through from the VAT increase and continued
growth in regulated domestic tariffs should keep monthly CPI elevated. However, the strong
tenge and a more moderate fiscal stance should help contain inflationary pressures compared
with last year.
As a result, we expect headline inflation to ease from 9.8% YoY in August to 9.5% in
September, largely due to favourable base effects. Following the NBK's front-loaded rate cut to

16.25% in September, we do not expect this moderation alone to provide a strong signal for
additional easing before year-end, especially given persistent external inflation risks and
uncertainty surrounding global commodity prices.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST)

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác 
tăng bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế 
gia tăng được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 
182 điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp từ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất và 6 điểm mới tại 
T2 Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Quốc và Gia Bình tiếp tục mở 
ra dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh được kỳ vọng 
tác động tích cực lên biên lợi nhuận của AST. Chúng tôi dự phóng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp đạt 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 
48%/17% yoy.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với AST, giá mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco là một 
trong những doanh nghiệp có quy mô mạng 
lưới điểm kinh doanh lớn trong lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ phi hàng không tại Việt Nam, với 141 
điểm kinh doanh tại 6 sân bay quốc tế tính 
đến cuối tháng 6/2026. Hoạt động cốt lõi của 
công ty bao gồm bán lẻ bách hóa lưu niệm 
(BHLN), hàng miễn thuế, dịch vụ ăn uống và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Ngoài ra, AST còn 
tham gia lĩnh vực suất ăn hàng không thông 
qua khoản đầu tư tại công ty liên kết VinaCS. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030.

điểm và tăng lên 182 điểm vào 2030.

Biểu đồ 5. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh của AST

Nguồn:AST

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng tăng trưởng lưu lượng hành khách, đặc biệt là sự gia tăng tỷ 
trọng khách quốc tế, sẽ thúc đẩy doanh thu và hiệu quả kinh doanh của AST 
trong giai đoạn 2026–2030. Theo Cục Hàng không Việt Nam, lượng khách thông 
qua các cảng hàng không trong 7T2026 đạt hơn 76 triệu lượt (+7% yoy), trong 
đó khách quốc tế đạt gần 30 triệu lượt (+11% yoy), trong khi khách nội địa ghi 
nhận tăng trưởng 4% yoy. Xu hướng này được hỗ trợ bởi nhu cầu du lịch quốc 
tế, chính sách thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng bay, trong khi tăng 
trưởng kinh tế và thu nhập tiếp tục tạo nền tảng cho nhu cầu đi lại trong nước. 
Trên cơ sở đó, chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế và nội địa tại các thị 
trường AST khai thác tăng bình quân lần lượt 11% và 5%/năm đến 2030, qua đó 
nâng dần tỷ trọng khách quốc tế trong tổng lưu lượng, thúc đẩy doanh 
thu/điểm kinh doanh nhờ: (1) lưu lượng hành khách qua cảng hàng không gia 
tăng và (2) tỷ trọng khách quốc tế cao hơn hỗ trợ doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Mở rộng mạng lưới tại các nhà ga 
hiện hữu và sân bay mới, gia tăng 
quy mô kinh doanh đến 2030 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng chu kỳ mở rộng hạ tầng hàng không giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ 
tạo dư địa gia tăng quy mô kinh doanh cho AST, với số điểm kinh doanh dự 
phóng đạt 145 điểm vào cuối 2026 và tăng lên 182 điểm vào năm 2030. Cụ thể: 
- Trong ngắn hạn, tăng trưởng năm 2026 được hỗ trợ bởi việc ghi nhận 
đủ năm doanh thu từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
trong năm 2025, cùng 6 điểm mới tại T2 Nội Bài trong năm 2026, trong 
đó có 2 cửa hàng miễn thuế và 2 cửa hàng Memories of Vietnam khai 
trương trong 2Q2026. 
- Trong trung và dài hạn, các dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành, Cảng HKQT 
Phú Quốc nâng cấp mở rộng và Cảng HKQT Gia Bình sẽ góp phần thúc 
đẩy ngành hàng không Việt Nam nói chung, bổ sung mặt bằng thương 
mại, mở ra cơ hội phát triển các mảng kinh doanh cốt lõi cho AST nói 
riêng bao gồm các mảng bán lẻ, miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Với kinh nghiệm khai thác 141 điểm kinh doanh 
tại 6 sân bay quốc tế và lịch sử trúng thầu tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất, T2 Nội 
Bài trong thời gian qua, chúng tôi kỳ vọng AST có khả năng cạnh tranh 
cao trong việc đấu thầu quyền khai thác mặt bằng tại các sân bay mới, 
đặc biệt là Gia Bình, qua đó duy trì vị thế và thị phần kinh doanh phi 
hàng không tại khu vực phía Bắc khi một phần lưu lượng hành khách 
được phân bổ lại từ Nội Bài.

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ mở rộng biên lợi nhuận  
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng biên lợi nhuận của AST tiếp tục cải thiện trong 2026 - 2027, 
nhờ định hướng mở rộng các mảng có biên lợi nhuận tốt như hàng miễn thuế 
và phòng chờ thương gia, kết hợp tối ưu hiệu quả vận hành tại các điểm kinh 
doanh. Cụ thể: (1) AST ưu tiên gia tăng số điểm kinh doanh hàng miễn thuế 
nhằm đón đầu tăng trưởng nhanh và ổn định của lượng khách quốc tế, qua đó 
nâng đóng góp lợi nhuận từ hoạt động mua sắm tại sân bay, (2) mở rộng mạng 
lưới phòng chờ, đồng thời gia tăng lượng khách sử dụng trên nền năng lực phục 
vụ hiện hữu và (3) tối ưu hóa hiệu quả hoạt động thông qua cải thiện bố trí mặt 
bằng, danh mục sản phẩm và kiểm soát chi phí tại các điểm kinh doanh, qua 
đó tăng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thu được trên mỗi khách hàng.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá 
mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu AST, với giá mục tiêu 76,500 
VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 
21/09/2026, dựa trên triển vọng tăng trưởng từ lượng khách quốc tế, mở rộng 
mạng lưới điểm kinh doanh và khả năng cải thiện biên lợi nhuận của doanh 
nghiệp. Chúng tôi sử dụng phương pháp định giá từng phần (SOTP), bao gồm 
DCF cho hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi của AST và P/E mục tiêu 11x đối với phần 
lợi nhuận thuộc sở hữu AST tại VinaCS. Các giả định chính gồm: (1) lượng khách 
quốc tế/nội địa tăng trung bình 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030, (2) số điểm kinh 
doanh tăng từ 145 điểm cuối 2026 lên 182 điểm năm 2030, với giả định AST 
trúng thầu mặt bằng tại cả Long Thành và Gia Bình và (3) biên lợi nhuận gộp 
2026/2027 đạt 64.7/65.5%. Đối với VinaCS, chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhà máy suất ăn 
Long Thành sẽ hỗ trợ tăng trưởng lợi nhuận trong trung và dài hạn khi dự kiến 
có thể bắt đầu đóng góp lợi nhuận cho AST từ 2028.

Rủi ro đối với định giá bao gồm: (1) AST không giành được quyền khai thác tại 
Long Thành và Gia Bình như kỳ vọng, (2) lưu lượng khách dịch chuyển giữa các 
sân bay mới và hiện hữu khiến doanh thu tại một số điểm kinh doanh bị sụt 
giảm, (3) cạnh tranh gia tăng từ các doanh nghiệp kinh doanh dịch vụ phi hàng 
không khác và (4) căng thẳng địa chính trị kéo dài, giá dầu tăng gây áp lực lên 
hoạt động hàng không và chi tiêu của hành khách.

## Document 278: Meta CTO解释Muse：回归人性交互，下一代操纵系统的核心是“意图理解”
